# 16: Chapter 16: Special Cuisine, Caterpie's Transformation

It had just rained, making the room feel a bit damp.

D turned and asked the three Pokémon, "Are you hungry?"

"Gao!"

"Gah-sii!"

"Ssss-oo~"

Cyclizar, Koraidon, and Caterpie all shook their heads.

They had barely moved all day and hadn't digested their lunch yet, so they weren't hungry.

Seeing this, D said, "Then I'll cook a bit later."

Before it got dark, he went to the storage room behind the house to start working.

With the help of Koraidon and Cyclizar, D chopped some of the wood he had collected earlier with an axe, preparing to throw it into the fireplace later. It would offer warmth and could also help fight the dampness.

In addition, D took out nails and wood, making a simple workbench in the storage room. Then he sawed the wood into planks and sticks.

Finally, he combined the two, creating a simple signboard.

D measured the size of the signboard, then handed it to Cyclizar to hold, planning to take it back to the room later to paint crop illustrations on it for marking farmland plots.

Finally, D crafted two more wooden sticks and took them back to the front bedroom.

"Caterpie, can you give me some bug silk?" D asked.

"Ssss-oo~"

Caterpie quickly spun out a strand of bug silk, and D wrapped it around the two wooden sticks. Instantly, a simple clothes hanger was ready.

"Feels pretty good, now there's a place to hang clothes,"

D clapped his hands together and stood up. Seeing it was already seven o'clock, he said to the three Pokémon, "Wait here, I'll go make dinner!"

Before starting to cook, D put the firewood into the fireplace and lit it.

The flames flickered, and soon the room began to gradually warm up.

D took off his coat, wearing just a shirt, and went to the kitchen area to start preparing dinner.

Tonight's dinner was grilled fish, specifically rainbow trout, a fish with tender, delicious, and aroma-free meat, with no small bones, making it very suitable for charcoal grilling.

D prepared the fish, then applied a thin layer of cooking oil on the surface, and finally pan-fried it until both sides were golden.

When it was almost done, he took the fish out and stuffed a mixture of sauce into its split belly, then grilled it over an open flame.

Soon, an enticing aroma filled the room.

Koraidon and Cyclizar, who had been lazily yawning, perked up when they smelled the fragrance, eagerly looking over.

D placed the grilled fish on a plate, with golden oil slowly dripping down its surface. Under the crispy golden skin was tender meat resembling red velvet, and the fragrance of the sauce filled the air, making one's mouth water.

He looked down, and the information about this dish popped up before him.

[Grilled Rainbow Trout (B): A simple charcoal-grilled delicacy preserving the original flavor of the fish, an irresistible dish.

Cooking Effect: Life Recovery (Extra Large), instantly restores vitality and energy to peak condition.

Evaluation: A dish with a primitive flavor, highly nutritious, suitable for all ages.]

Life Recovery?

Equivalent to a Max Potion plus a bottle of comprehensive PP restoration effects.

Overall, not bad.

Smelling this aroma, D couldn't help but take a quick bite. At the moment of entry, he first tasted the crispy fish skin, followed by the tender fish meat.

The fish meat was so tender it practically melted in his mouth. The distinct layered texture was hard to resist.

D couldn't help but want to take another bite, but suddenly felt two scorching gazes on him.

Looking up, he saw Cyclizar and Koraidon eagerly watching, drooling.

D: "..."

He turned and placed two more fish in front of them, helplessly saying, "Take it easy; you're drooling all over the floor."

Cyclizar and Koraidon began feasting.

D then looked at Caterpie.

However, Caterpie seemed uninterested in the grilled fish.

"You're not eating?"

"Ssss-oo..."

Caterpie shook its head.

Seeing this, D suddenly remembered that fish didn't seem to be in Caterpie's diet.

They preferred fresh green leaves and juicy berries over meat, with huge appetites capable of eating a hundred leaves a day.

"Alright." D turned around, took out some berries from the cupboard, and cut them into small pieces to make a Berry Platter for Caterpie.

He placed the Berry Platter in front of Caterpie, and Caterpie's eyes lit up with joy before it eagerly started eating.

Seeing it eat with such relish, D also took a look at the effect of the Berry Platter.

[Berry Platter (C-): A dish made with various berries, blending different berry flavors, popular with some Pokémon.

Cooking Effect: Skill Refinement, after eating, a Pokémon's move will randomly change.

Evaluation: Basic cooking, easy to make, is a good choice for home and travel.]

"Pfft!"

D nearly spat out a mouthful of water.

It happened!

This simple dish of Berry Platter actually had such a special effect!

Skill Refinement, which sounded advanced, but in reality, was a double-edged sword that tested one's luck.

Commonly known as "playing by chance."

In the game, when players fed it to their characters, the skills they had could undergo random changes.

There were five changes, namely:

1. Skill level increase, increasing power.

2. Skill level decrease, reducing power.

3. Forgetting the skill.

4. Skill specialization, causing a special change in the skill.

5. Learning a new skill.

In his previous life, because of this cooking effect, the game company he worked for was nearly overwhelmed by player complaints.

Many players had eagerly hoped to enhance their characters' skills, but after feeding them, were shocked to find skills were not enhanced but forgotten instead.

That player, unwilling to give up, fed a dozen more times, only to create a completely skill-less character in the game.

Furious, that player almost showed up at the company with a knife to confront the developers.

Thinking of this, D looked at the berries devoured by Caterpie.

It was too late to stop it now.

Patiently waiting for Caterpie to finish eating, D tentatively asked, "Caterpie, do you feel any changes in your body? Like in your moves or anything..."

"Ssss-oo..."

Upon hearing this, Caterpie appeared puzzled at first, then began to carefully sense changes in its body.

Soon, it seemed to have discovered something, and a bright bulb appeared above its head: "Ssss-oo!"

"Hmm?"

Caterpie took a deep breath and formed an O-shape with its mouth.

Subsequently, a net made of white bug silk shot out,.

Unlike normal bug silk, the net sparkled with strands of yellow electricity!

Seeing this, D inhaled sharply.

Because he already knew what had changed in Caterpie.

The Berry Platter had made it learn Electroweb!

---

---

---

---

# 17: Chapter 17: The Cyclizar of the Past, the Koraidon of the Future

Electroweb, catch the opponent with an electrically charged web, also capable of reducing the opponent's speed.

Have to say, this Caterpie's luck is unbelievably good.

Caterpie was quite surprised by its own transformation.

From the events of the past two days, it roughly understood that its changes were due to food.

Food can make it stronger!

Caterpie immediately looked at D expectantly.

"No way!" D shook his head: "Too much greed leads to no gain. Master the move you understood today before trying anything new!"

Besides, the more important point is that the cooking effect of the [Berry Platter] is uncontrollable.

No one can guarantee what might happen after continuous consumption.

This time it got lucky and learned Electroweb, but next time, it might experience bad luck and forget all its moves completely.

"Hiss..."

Caterpie thought about it and realized that what he said made perfect sense, so it immediately decided to practice this newly learned powerful move diligently, to master it completely.

Only then can it become stronger!

Caterpie was filled with determination.

But before that, it had to properly thank D.

"Hiss~"

Caterpie affectionately tapped D's arm with its antenna, expressing its gratitude.

D smiled and said to it, "Go rest for a bit!"

Caterpie obediently nodded, turned around, and moved to a corner to rest.

While D looked down at the empty white porcelain plate.

"I must be cautious in the future, can't make this dish casually."

D kept this in mind, worried that he might turn Cyclizar and Koraidon into idiots.

Koraidon and Cyclizar, after eating grilled fish, showed a satisfied expression, sitting on the ground licking the corners of their mouths with their tongues.

D glanced at their foolish look and couldn't help but smile.

During the rainy night, the temperature dropped, making it chilly outside.

Thanks to the lit fireplace, the room was very warm and cozy everywhere.

The three well-fed Pokemon were in such a warm environment, soon yawning and getting drowsy.

Having lazed around all day, D wasn't very sleepy at the moment.

He moved a wooden stool in front of the fireplace, sat down, and began drawing on the signposts, based on the designs in the "Pasture Encyclopedia," for the crops he had planted.

D carved with great dedication.

The flickering flames in the fireplace outlined his shadow, softly shimmering on the wall behind him.

Caterpie was already snoring.

Resting beside the bed, Cyclizar lifted its head to look this way, noticing D hadn't come over to sleep for a long time, it got up and moved closer.

The firelight cast a red glow on Cyclizar's body, and D turned his head to glance at it.

"Gyaoo~"

Cyclizar yawned, then obediently lay down beside D, accompanying him in his work.

Seeing this, D felt a warmth in his heart, smiled, and was about to continue his work when he heard Koraidon's footsteps behind him.

Turning his head, he saw Koraidon coming over and lying down on his other side, just like Cyclizar.

Koraidon looked displeased at Cyclizar.

Damn it, it got ahead of me!

Staying by the master's side, Cyclizar completely relaxed, closing its eyes and falling asleep peacefully, not noticing Koraidon's gaze at all.

Seeing this, Koraidon felt like it punched a pillow.

Koraidon sighed, lay on the floor, resting its head on its front paws, and past memories floated in its mind.

It recalled not long after it arrived by D's side, it also encountered a Koraidon.

At that time, it didn't know, but thinking about it now, that Koraidon should have been itself.

Later on, as a Cyclizar, it fell into a Time Machine and was transformed into Koraidon by mysterious forces.

So, the question arises, will this Cyclizar, like its former self, turn into Koraidon?

Thinking about it, Koraidon felt its head getting muddled like paste.

It didn't know why it turned out this way, nor could it understand, even struggling to distinguish who was who.

Is it Koraidon... or Cyclizar?

Koraidon got itself all confused, feeling like stars were spinning above its head, finally giving up thinking about it.

Anyhow, as long as it can return to D, that's all that matters.

Hearing Koraidon's sigh beside him, D turned his head and couldn't help but ask, "Why are you suddenly sighing?"

Could King Wings also have something troubling?

Could it be because it lost to another Koraidon in territorial disputes?

Recalling the game's storyline, D comforted, "Don't worry, when you recover, you'll surely defeat that guy."

Although he didn't have Secret Spice, the dishes he made shouldn't be too bad, there's bound to be a dish that can help Koraidon regain its strength.

D was full of confidence, but Koraidon was filled with questions.

"Gawoo?"

D encouraged, "Don't overthink it, we'll definitely find another way!"

"Gyhiss~"

Seeing D's demeanor, Koraidon couldn't help but feel happy.

It leaned forward, and then slobbered D's face with a set of licks.

D struggled to push it away, purposely putting on a stern face, "Don't just lick people whenever you like in the future."

Seeing D angry, Koraidon's face immediately showed a pitiful expression.

D: "..."

Sigh, whatever, let it be!

————

At dawn the next day, the fireplace had already gone out.

The rain had stopped, and the sky had returned to its usual clarity.

D stepped out of the wooden house, breathing in the fresh air outside, immediately feeling refreshed.

He stretched, ready to start a new day!

Putting on shoes and holding the prepared signposts, D went to the farmland and placed the signposts in front of the corresponding crops.

Just after the rain, large and small water droplets still clung to the leaves of the trees.

While D was working, Caterpie came outside, crawled up a big tree, and started eating fresh leaves while watching D and Cyclizar and Koraidon helping nearby.

It must work hard to get stronger, not only to prove to those Pokemon who looked down on it that it is not weak, but also to thank D. Only by evolving into the most powerful Butterfree can it truly lend D a hand!

So, after eating a few leaves, Caterpie started practicing its Electroweb by itself.

With the signposts in place, D took Koraidon and Cyclizar to the west of the pasture.

That place hadn't been taken care of yet, with weeds and trees growing everywhere, appearing very messy.

Coming over this time, D planned to cut down all the trees in this area to build the Chicken House.

He had saved enough funds for the construction, only needing wood and stone.

In at most two days, he'll be able to go to Porto Marinada Town and hire Keri at the carpenter shop to help build the Chicken House, starting the farming venture.

# 18: Chapter 18: The Unlucky Trainer

Five o'clock in the evening, Zack arrived on time to collect the goods.

However, today D had nothing to sell.

Perhaps Koraidon was too aggressive with frying fish the previous days, causing a significant reduction in fish in the nearby river, and he only caught three normal bass.

Zack seemed accustomed to this and instead started comforting D.

"This kind of thing is normal. Fishing is about luck. You've already done well, you know. Some people sit by the river all day without catching a single fish!"

D: "..."

Empty-handed, huh?

After selling the three bass and accepting the 600 League coins Zack handed him, D pondered whether to go fishing in the sea west of Porto Marinada Town.

The fish in the river were almost all caught, it might take a while for them to recover.

At this time, the crops on the farm hadn't matured yet, and besides fishing, he didn't have other ways to make money.

But D wasn't worried, because the most important thing for now was to sort out the Chicken House.

After washing himself and his two Pokemon with the tap water, D returned to the wooden house to prepare dinner.

The rainbow trout was gone, leaving only some smoked sausages and ham, which were common ready-to-eat foods at the Porto Marinada Market.

They were easy to store and would keep for days at room temperature, perfect for places without a refrigerator.

However, sandwiches made with smoked sausages and ham lacked any special effects.

Despite this, both Koraidon and Cyclizar were eating happily.

This made D think of an old saying from his previous life—A dog doesn't despise a poor home.

In this world, Pokemon are like domesticated dogs, once they accept you as their owner, they won't disdain you no matter how poor you are.

Moreover, Pokemon will live together with humans, and with the emotion and intelligence they possess, D thought it might be more appropriate to call them family.

As for Caterpie…

D knew it had been outside all day diligently practicing moves, and when it got tired, it would munch on fresh green leaves from the trees.

After a whole day of munching, it probably wasn't very hungry now.

Not even returning yet, D went out to take a look and found it had fallen asleep on a big tree in the farm.

Compared to the house, Caterpie seemed to prefer the big tree outside.

After dinner, a busy day led D to choose to go to bed early for rest.

At the same time, in the back mountain.

A blond boy, around ten years old, crawled out of the ditch in a sorry state.

The uniform with the Orange Academy emblem was covered in mud and damp, decaying leaves.

The boy looked at his dirty clothes with disdain, sitting on a tree with a face full of helplessness.

The boy's name was Carter, a first-year student of Orange Academy. He had left school two days prior to participate in an outdoor course, only to unexpectedly get lost along the way.

He initially planned to keep moving forward to see if he could find a place where people lived, but then it started to rain.

The rain lasted all day, trapping him in a cave for the entire day.

Once the rain stopped, he tried to get out and look around, only to fall into a ditch that not only dirtied his clothes but also scraped his leg.

With all his food eaten, Carter leaned against a tree, enduring hunger, his face filled with helplessness.

"I wonder if Delibird has found the location of the town. Ah, it's really annoying. If I knew this would happen, I wouldn't have come to this extracurricular activity."

In the afternoon, Carter released his only Pokemon, Delibird, and asked it to use its sense of direction from the air to find a place where people lived.

Suddenly, the sound of Delibird's voice came from the sky.

Carter quickly looked up, his face full of surprise.

Looking at Delibird landing in front of him, Carter urgently asked, "Did you find a town nearby?"

"Bibi!"

Delibird nodded while taking out a blue berry from its white bag and handing it to its Trainer.

"You specially found food for me?" Carter held the berry, tears of gratitude welling up, and hugged Delibird tightly: "Thank you so much, Delibird!"

"Bibi!"

Delibird was squeezed so tightly it almost couldn't breathe.

Fortunately, Carter soon let go, took a bite of the berry, and regained his energy: "Alright! Then let's continue on!"

"Bibi!"

With Delibird leading the way, Carter followed closely behind it.

After walking for about an hour, under the moonlight, a dark wooden house appeared ahead.

"Is that...a wooden house?" Carter felt a bit disappointed; he initially thought it would be a bustling human town.

Besides that, Carter also saw farmland; it seemed to be a farm.

"Bibi!"

Delibird nodded its head, indicating it had seen traces of human activities here before.

"That's still better than getting lost in the mountains." Carter thought as he knocked on the door: "Hello, sorry to bother you, is anyone living here?"

Soon, footsteps were heard inside.

The lights came on inside, and as the door opened, a tall and skinny young man appeared in the doorway.

D looked at the dirty boy in front of him, a flash of surprise crossing his face: "You are?"

Carter hurriedly introduced himself: "Hello, my name is Carter! I'm a student at Orange Academy, I accidentally got lost in the mountains... I wanted to ask, is the town far from here?"

D glanced at him briefly and said: "It takes about three hours to walk there."

"Ah? That far?" Carter was dumbfounded, not knowing what to do.

His stamina was severely depleted, and he probably couldn't make it there.

D looked at the emblem on Carter's chest, raised an eyebrow slightly, and then said: "Come in, you don't look like you can make it, you can stay here for the night."

Carter gratefully said: "Thank you so much! I won't stay for free, I will pay for the room!"

"Forget it, it's just a small gesture, no need for room fee."

From the original memory, D knew that hotel fees in this world were not high, the cheapest one was about two hundred a night.

Thinking about it, D stepped aside.

Hearing this, Carter is even more grateful.

Truly a good person!

He walked into the room with Delibird.

Judging from the outside, the wooden house wasn't large, but its interior was well-equipped.

A single bed, a desk, cabinets to partition the space, and a lit fireplace.

The wooden floors were spotless, and the room was impeccably clean and tidy.

"The owner of the house loves cleanliness," Carter thought.

Worried his shoes would dirty the house, Carter took them off before entering, stepping inside wearing only his long socks.

At this moment, his gaze suddenly landed on two Pokemon lying beside the bed.

A Cyclizar, and a completely crimson one, a stranger Pokemon he had never seen before.

Although he hadn't seen it before, his Trainer's intuition kept telling him that this Pokemon was extraordinary!

---

---

---

# 19: Chapter 19: Caterpie vs Delibird! (Two Chapters in One)

Carter's gaze was drawn to Koraidon.

D turned back and noticed his expression, followed his gaze, and saw Koraidon yawning lazily.

"Are you traveling outside now?" D asked.

Judging by his attire, he looked very much like those trainers D had seen in anime who were traveling.

Carter nodded, "Yes, by the way, how should I address you?"

Orange Academy's courses follow a free system.

They don't require students to attend classes daily; instead, the school encourages students to explore various parts of Paldea or challenge gyms to collect badges.

D understood and didn't ask more, "Just call me D. I've just taken over this abandoned ranch recently."

"Alright, Mr. D, sorry to bother you!" Carter noticed there was only one bed in the room, so he took a sleeping bag from his backpack and laid it on the floor.

His gaze occasionally fell on Koraidon, and he couldn't help but ask, "What Pokémon is that?"

The name Koraidon was given by Professor Sada when she discovered it, and at this time, Koraidon's name wasn't widely known yet.

Thinking of this, D pretended not to know Koraidon's name and shook his head, "I don't know. I've just encountered it, and it seems there is no information about it in the Pokedex."

Upon hearing this, Carter seemed very excited, "So it's an undiscovered Pokémon! Mr. D, have you caught it?"

"Caught?" D looked at Koraidon and said, "Sort of."

"Wow!" Carter's eyes were filled with admiration, "Mr. D, then you must be a great trainer, right?"

D: "...No, I'm just a rancher."

However, Carter didn't seem to believe him.

The aura of that Pokémon is very strong, and if D weren't a powerful trainer, how could he have caught it?

It is well-known that to capture a Pokémon, you first need to defeat it in battle, proving your strength.

Only then will you gain the Pokémon's recognition, and it will be willing to be captured in a Poke Ball.

D: "..."

He didn't know what to say, so he glanced at the time and, seeing it was late, chose to go to bed.

Carter had experienced a day of wind and rain outside and was both physically and mentally exhausted, so he obediently got into his sleeping bag and soon fell asleep.

In the silent night, the next morning, Carter woke up early.

After a good night's sleep, he felt refreshed all over.

He looked at the bed and saw that Mr. D was still asleep.

Carter quietly got up, took the Delibird's Poke Ball, and headed outside.

Standing under the eaves, the ranch he couldn't see clearly last night was finally visible to him.

Neatly arranged furrows, brown, soft soil, and signposts standing in front of the land.

From a distance, Carter couldn't see clearly, so he walked to the front of the farmland area.

Now he could finally see the patterns and words on those signs clearly.

"Turnip, Potato..." Carter read them aloud, and then thought, "Mr. D is a very meticulous person!"

It was apparent that this abandoned ranch had been organized neatly by him.

Everyone is making an effort.

Thinking of this, Carter picked up the Poke Ball, "Delibird, we can't be lazy either!"

With a flash of white light, Delibird appeared in mid-air.

"Before breakfast, let's have a good training session!" Carter said enthusiastically.

This was his way of training Delibird. Every morning after getting up, he would run a few laps with Delibird on the school's playground before going to have breakfast.

"Bibi!"

Delibird responded, then flapped its wings and began training around the ranch with his trainer.

After running a few laps, the pair stopped, resting under a tree at the edge of the ranch for a few minutes. Carter then said:

"Next, let's practice the moves you've mastered! Have you become proficient in that Icy Wind move you learned last time?"

"Bibi!"

Delibird nodded its head.

Just then, Carter noticed a wild Caterpie on a branch overhead.

His eyes lit up, and he commanded Delibird, "Delibird, let's spar with that Caterpie!"

"Bibi!"

Hearing the voice below, the Caterpie on the treetop calmly looked down.

Does it want to fight?

The Caterpie fearlessly gazed at Delibird.

Seeing the Caterpie ready for battle, Carter ordered, "Delibird, use Icy Wind!"

"Bibi!"

Upon receiving the command, Delibird quickly gathered Ice Type Energy on its body, flapping its wings as a blast of freezing air automatically formed and swept toward the Caterpie on the branch.

"Although Ice Type doesn't counter Caterpie, Delibird has trained hard for so long, the power of Icy Wind must have improved greatly compared to before. Defeating a Caterpie should be no problem."

It's well-known that Caterpie Pokémon are notoriously weak. When facing other Pokémon, besides spitting out fragile Bug Silk, they have almost no ability to retaliate.

---

Watching the Icy Wind sweeping out, Carter couldn't help but think.

However, in the next second, that Caterpie made a move beyond expectation.

It calmly looked at the Icy Wind, and then quickly spat out Bug Silk from its mouth to block in front of it.

The amount of Bug Silk was astonishing, forming a seamless web in just a few breaths.

The Icy Wind hit the web, freezing it into an ice block, but the umbrella-like ice block perfectly shielded the Caterpie behind it.

"What?"

Carter was slightly taken aback.

In the moment he was stunned, the web frozen into an ice block fell to the ground, and immediately, a large net sparking with yellow currents shot out from behind.

Before Delibird could react, it was hit by the Electroweb.

Threads of electric current spread out, and Delibird let out a painful Growl, then rolled its eyes back and lost the ability to fight.

Seeing this scene, Carter was frozen in place, his face revealing a look of disbelief.

If he wasn't mistaken, the move just used by the Caterpie was Electroweb?

But how is that possible?!

Is this still the Caterpie he knows?

At that moment, footsteps came from behind.

Carter turned around and saw D walking over.

He was about to say something when he saw the wild Caterpie on the tree jump down and come to D's side.

Carter's eyes widened, and he changed his words midway: "Mr. D... is this Caterpie your Pokemon?"

"Yes." D nodded, looked at the unconscious Delibird, and raised an eyebrow: "Did you just battle with it?"

Looking at the state of that Delibird, it should have been defeated by Caterpie with Electroweb.

Electric Type is doubly effective against Flying Type, and combined with Caterpie's unexpected attack, it's not surprising that it defeated Delibird.

Hearing this, the young master from the city blushed immediately and apologized hastily: "I'm very sorry! I thought it was a wild Pokemon!"

"..." D was about to speak when suddenly, a strong milky white glow burst from the Caterpie beside him.

In the light, Caterpie's body was rapidly changing.

The originally soft body became hard as iron, the legs underneath disappeared, becoming smooth, the body becoming crescent-shaped, with a green protrusion on the back, and the black eyes half open and half closed.

When the white light dissipated, the original Caterpie disappeared, replaced by a brand-new Metapod.

Watching this scene, Carter opened his mouth in surprise: "It evolved!"

D smiled slightly: "I should thank you for helping Caterpie complete its evolution."

"No, it's not my credit!" Carter said, somewhat embarrassed.

D chuckled, picked up Metapod in his arms, and walked towards the wooden house: "Let's go back first! Your Delibird seems to need treatment."

Carter followed along.

D looked down at Caterpie and said to it: "Congratulations, you've successfully evolved."

Metapod couldn't speak or move, and could only use Harden.

Normally, wild Caterpies find a safe place before evolution, hang themselves on trees by spinning Bug Silk, and then begin to evolve.

Therefore, just as Caterpie felt it was about to evolve, it immediately ran to D's side.

For D, Caterpie had great trust. It believed he would take it to a safe place.

Sure enough, as it expected, D led it back to the wooden house.

Considering the environment Caterpie likes, before settling it down, D asked: "Do you want to stay outside or in the room? If outside, blink."

Metapod blinked.

D nodded: "Okay, I'll make a box for you with planks later."

Metapod couldn't move and could only look over with grateful eyes.

D temporarily placed the Caterpie on a blanket, then turned to check on Carter's Delibird.

Delibird had fainted, its body twitching slightly, seemingly affected by Electric Type Energy.

"Feed it a mixture of a Cheri Berry and an Oran Berry, and it should recover." With that, D stood up, found the two berries from the basket, mixed them, and fed them to Delibird.

Soon, Delibird groggily opened its eyes and recovered.

Seeing this, Carter breathed a sigh of relief, but then he thought of Caterpie's earlier performance and asked in shock: "Mr. D, how did you do it?"

D was slightly surprised: "What?"

Carter: "How did you train Caterpie to that level?"

A Bug Type Caterpie being able to defeat a Flying Type Delibird with one move.

What's more unbelievable, that Caterpie could use a special move like Electroweb.

Caterpie couldn't possibly learn that move on its own unless it had been given special training.

But that's extremely difficult; a novice Trainer could never achieve such a feat.

Not to mention Caterpie doesn't have Electric Type Energy; teaching it Electroweb requires some kind of Secret Moves by the Trainer.

Even an Elite Level Trainer might not succeed!

---

Catching a mysterious and powerful Pokemon, teaching a weak little Caterpie to learn Electroweb...

Mr. D must be a very skillful Trainer!

Carter looked over, visibly excited.

D: "..."

He put on an awkward yet polite smile, "That was all the Caterpie's own effort, it's a very special Pokemon."

This wasn't wrong, apart from feeding Caterpie a few dishes, D hadn't offered any other Helping Hand, it was all its own hard work.

To become a strong Pokemon, effort and luck are both indispensable.

However, Carter had a face full of "I understand, I understand" expression.

After all, these are Secret Moves that only Mr. D has mastered.

D didn't know what this kid was thinking, but from his expression and eyes, it seemed he had imagined some strange things.

Not wanting to continue this topic, he got up to prepare breakfast.

For today's breakfast, D didn't choose to cook himself, instead, he took a lazy route by making a simple sandwich with smoked sausage and tomatoes purchased from Porto Marinada Market.

Nonetheless, Koraidon and Cyclizar still enjoyed it happily.

Carter and Delibird also seemed very grateful.

After breakfast, Carter got ready to leave the ranch, heading to the nearest town to replenish supplies, and then planned to return to Orange Academy.

With Delibird, he stood at the ranch's exit and waved goodbye to D, "Goodbye, Mr. D! Thank you so much for your hospitality! I will always remember you! Whenever I get a chance, I'll come visit you with Delibird!"

D nodded slightly, "Go!"

Carter waved energetically, then turned and ran towards the direction of Porto Marinada Town.

D stood in place watching his back, suddenly feeling a bit like the side characters Ash encounters in anime.

Sometimes providing help to passing Trainers, chatting with them about travels and Pokemon, after brief encounters they part ways, each continuing their journey in life.

Thinking of this, D shook his head and smiled to himself.

Until Carter's figure could no longer be seen, he turned around and went to the utility room to start making a nest for Metapod.

He used a saw to cut the wood into planks, then took out some nails and nailed the planks into an open-topped wooden box.

Returning to the front cabin, D laid fresh leaves inside, placed two Berries inside, then put Metapod in it, finally stringing the wooden box with rope and hanging it under the eaves where it wouldn't get rained on.

The open-topped box allowed Metapod to see the outside world, and also facilitated airflow.

After evolving from Caterpie to Metapod, Metapod no longer needed to grow through battles.

The entire Metapod stage is for getting ready for evolution, its hard shell protecting Metapod inside, while the body grows by constantly renewing cells.

Once it grows to a certain degree, Metapod will break out of its shell, transform into a butterfly, evolving into its final form, Butterfree.

D looked forward to that day.

Thanks to the influence of Ash's Butterfree, D also holds a bit of a childhood filter for this kind of Pokemon.

"Grow up quickly!"

D reached out and touched Metapod's shell.

As if sensing his hopes, Metapod used Harden, its body shimmering with a translucent green glow, responding to D's words.

It heard him.

---

---

---

---

# 20: Chapter 20: Building the Chicken House

Two days later, the trees and rocks in the ranch were thoroughly cleared, leaving only a patch of uneven weeds.

Cyclizar was playing in the grass, while Koraidon stood beside D.

During this time, the two had been in a state of mutual non-interference.

Because Koraidon didn't know how to face its past self, it kept a certain distance from Cyclizar.

Cyclizar was puzzled, and the occasional disdainful and envious looks from Koraidon, along with the intimidating aura it exuded, made Cyclizar hesitant to approach.

D was unaware of the situation between the two Pokemon; he had been very busy in recent days, immersed in the ranch work.

At that moment, D stood under the eaves, gazing at the ranch he had worked so hard to manage.

Without the trees obstructing the view, the ranch looked much more open, allowing him to take in the entire ranch from the location of the cabin.

Finally, it's time!

D clicked his tongue in amazement and turned around to check the lumber behind the cabin. Seeing there was enough, he prepared to head to Porto Marinada Town to ask Keri from the carpentry shop to help build the Chicken House.

Riding on Cyclizar, D looked at Koraidon beside him and said, "I'll leave the task of guarding the house to you!"

"Gawu..."

Koraidon put on a pitiful look, enviously eyeing Cyclizar.

D: "..."

He reached out to pat Koraidon's head and said, "Be good, and next time I'll take you out!"

"Gaow!"

Koraidon immediately brightened up, licking D's hand like a big puppy.

Leaving Koraidon to guard the ranch, D gently patted Cyclizar's shoulder, and Cyclizar started to run.

Koraidon and the ranch gradually disappeared behind them, and D only looked back once they were out of sight, thinking:

"Not having a Poke Ball is indeed a bit inconvenient; I can't take my Pokemon with me at all times."

Although it's not impossible to take Koraidon along, doing so would be too conspicuous. D preferred to keep a low profile.

Poke Balls aren't expensive; he could buy a few as spares while in town, allowing Koraidon and the others to run freely on the ranch.

When needing to go out, they could be stored in Poke Balls and carried along.

Therefore, upon arriving in Porto Marinada Town, D headed straight to a store specializing in Trainer supplies and bought a few standard red and white balls.

After that, he went to Porto Marinada Market to buy ingredients for the next period, and finally headed to the carpentry shop.

From a distance, D saw the shop owner, Keri.

Today, she wore a khaki jumpsuit over a pink shirt, her pink hair tied up high, with her bangs held up by a headband, revealing a smooth forehead.

Keri's face was a classic oval shape; perhaps due to years of woodworking, she had a robust build, giving off an impression of being straightforward and capable.

Keri was sawing a wooden plank on a stump at the entrance.

Upon hearing footsteps behind her, she turned around and immediately recognized the young rancher.

"It's you? The weather is great today, how's the work at the ranch going?" Keri asked with a smile.

"It's almost done, Ms. Keri," D said, explaining his purpose: "I'd like to ask for your help in building a Chicken House."

Keri looked surprised, "So soon? It seems like it's been less than a week since we last met? You've already saved up for the expansion?"

D nodded with a smile, "Thanks to the help of Cyclizar and the others, so the progress was much quicker."

Keri glanced at Cyclizar, understanding, "I see, please wait a moment!"

She put down the saw and went inside, quickly returning with measuring tools and an adult Cyclizar.

"Let's go! Take me to your ranch. I need to survey the site. Once you finalize the location for the Chicken House, I'll come with a construction team to start working tomorrow morning."

"Sure."

D nodded, leading the way on his Cyclizar, with Keri following behind, arriving at the ranch after about half an hour.

From afar, D saw Koraidon waiting at the ranch entrance for his return.

Koraidon, who had been lying bored on the grass, jumped up excitedly upon seeing D.

Keri also noticed the Pokemon, one she had never seen before.

"Is it also your Pokemon?"

Keri was not a Trainer; her knowledge of Pokemon was limited to those inhabiting the surroundings of Porto Marinada Town and the ones she occasionally saw on TV.

Therefore, Koraidon was just an unfamiliar Pokemon to her, not warranting much surprise.

"Yes, thanks to its help, I was able to gather materials so quickly." D nodded with a smile.

Hearing this, Keri did not ask further questions.

They got off Cyclizar at the ranch entrance, and D soothingly patted Koraidon's head, then walked alongside Keri around the ranch.

Cyclizar and Koraidon followed slowly behind them.

Admiring the scene before her, Keri praised, "You've kept this place very well!"

She fell into thought, "I visited here once when I was young. My parents specifically brought me to experience milking cows. Back then, the owner of this ranch was a kind old man who warmly welcomed us."

"He was my grandfather," D said, recounting from the original owner's memories: "Because my parents had a special illness that required rest in a good environment, we moved to the Hoenn Region when I was very young."

"I see, no wonder I didn't see you then." Keri nodded lightly, then asked, "Have you chosen a location for the Chicken House?"

D pointed to a piece of empty land on the west side of the farmland and said, "There."

That spot, near a river, would be ideal for enclosing with a fence once the Chicken House is built, allowing the chickens to roam and exercise on sunny days.

Keri took a glance and, from a carpenter's perspective, commented that it was indeed a fine spot:

"A great choice, besides the Chicken House, you can also build a water-powered mill by the river to grind the wheat and corn grown here using wind and water power. The resulting flour and cornmeal can be used for your own consumption or as chicken feed."

D was intrigued, "A water mill?" Is that a free addition?

Seeing his thought, Keri chuckled, "That's an additional cost. Once you have enough funds, I'll come over to build it for you."

D: "..."

Having confirmed the location, Keri took out her tools for measurement. After collecting the data, she wandered around the ranch with D leading the way.

Since future collaboration would be necessary, an upfront survey of the ranch was crucial, and she could also assist in planning the ranch.

---

---

---

---

# 21: Chapter 21: Rancher? Capitalist!

The two arrived at the farmland area. Keri squatted down to inspect the surroundings and then said, "The soil here is very good, quite suitable for planting crops."

D recalled a ranch game he had played before and asked, "Miss Keri, do you have any automatic watering machines? The kind you just stick into the ground, and it automatically waters?"

Like an automatic sprinkler. As the ranch grows bigger and more crops are planted, there's a chance he might not be able to handle it all by himself.

At such times, having an automatic sprinkler would be great. It would water the nearby plots every morning, saving both effort and worry.

However, the ideal is full, but reality is quite skinny.

Keri was somewhat helpless: "Where would you find such a thing!"

D: "..." As expected, a game is just a game.

Keri: "But there is another type of automated device."

"Hmm?"

Keri: "Lay underground pipes in advance, set up a faucet, and then use precise instruments to time it. At the designated time, it will automatically spray water around for irrigation.

The kind where you plant it in the ground and it produces water out of nowhere simply doesn't exist in our era."

Hearing this, D had a thought: "Can your shop make it?"

"Of course," Keri raised her eyebrows and said, "but the price is quite high! A set that covers the entire farmland costs around thirty to forty thousand League coins. The pipes laid underground, the water pump, and timing instruments aren't very expensive individually, but if the number is excessive..."

D understood.

Thirty to forty thousand League coins isn't much, but it's clearly more than he can afford right now.

Realizing his predicament, Keri added, "Of course, there's another method for watering."

"What?" D was slightly taken aback.

Keri smiled: "Water-type Pokémon can use their Water-type moves to help with watering."

"In the city, some wild Pokémon also collaborate with humans, being hired for work like delivering letters with Pelipper, construction work with Conkeldurr, and moving with Machamp. I suppose you've seen them?"

D hadn't seen them, but the original owner had.

He nodded.

Keri continued: "Pokémon have simple needs. As long as we can provide enough food and snacks, they are willing to help us work. This is why the relationship between humans and Pokémon has grown increasingly harmonious over the years."

No need to search for food in the wild anymore or fight other Pokémon for Berries, and they get a shelter against the wind and rain.

For Pokémon, what could be more delightful?

As the saying goes, Ogres shall never be slaves, unless they are provided food and lodging.

Compared to the former, this method of watering is clearly much cheaper.

He only needed to pay a Berry, a bottle of milk, a bit of food, and he could have a free and obedient labor force...

D was very tempted.

Wouldn't this mean he could hire hundreds of Pokémon to help manage the ranch, and he could just comfortably sit on the couch waiting for the money to roll in?

Damn, this is such capitalist behavior!

D quickly shook his head to get rid of such a frightening thought.

He couldn't help but ask: "Wouldn't that lead to some people forcing Pokémon to work?"

Keri: "Regarding that, it depends on the person hiring the Pokémon. If they treat Pokémon as tools, the Pokémon will sense it, feel angry and upset, and either attack the employer or refuse to work and return to the wild."

"If the employer uses force to suppress the Pokémon and makes them work—well, that's strictly prohibited by the League. If such a thing occurs, Miss Jenny would immediately arrest them."

"If someone treats Pokémon as partners, friends, or even family, working and living together, I think, even if the work is difficult, the Pokémon would strive to complete it together with them."

D understood.

He nodded thoughtfully.

Seeing this, Keri smiled slightly and continued to stroll through the ranch.

The ranch, other than the farmland, was empty. Oh, not quite right.

Behind the log cabin was a small cave.

The cave wasn't large, and its environment was dark and damp, quite cool. It's a good place to cool off in the summer.

Keri walked into the cave, looked at the scene inside, and then said, "This place is quite suitable for growing mushrooms!"

D also thought so; indeed, great minds think alike.

Keri: "Coincidentally, I have mushroom cultivation trays at my place. Placed in such a damp and dark environment, you can harvest different edible mushrooms daily. Do you need some? They're not expensive, only 5000 League coins."

5000...

The price really isn't high.

If he can grow mushrooms himself, he wouldn't need to buy them in town.

D nodded, indicating he'd buy them!

Seeing this, Keri smiled: "Alright, I'll bring the mushroom cultivation trays along with the construction crew tomorrow."

"Then I'll be waiting here!" D said.

Keri gently nodded. Seeing that it was getting late, she didn't stay longer. She rode her Cyclizar back to town.

The next morning, as D was making breakfast, he heard the sound of a truck starting outside.

Stepping out, he saw a truck loaded with tools parked at the ranch.

Keri alighted from the truck along with several Machamp and Conkeldurr.

Seeing D, Keri greeted, "Good morning, D!"

D also nodded lightly, "Good morning, Miss Keri!"

Keri laughed and said, "The construction work starts today. It will take about three days to complete, so you don't need to worry about us until then."

"Alright."

Though she said so, D couldn't contain his curiosity.

He took a sandwich and, with Koraidon and Cyclizar, sat under the eaves watching the construction team work.

Under Keri's direction, Machamp and Conkeldurr went to the back to haul out all the wood and stones D had prepared.

During this, Keri moved a few wooden boxes filled with soil into the cave behind the log cabin: "These are the mushroom cultivation trays. Put them in the cave, and the mushrooms will grow on their own. I'm moving them in now!"

"Alright, thank you!" D said.

Keri smiled. After all the wooden boxes were moved, she began to work with Machamp and Conkeldurr again.

Some were sawing and chiseling the wood and stones, while others mixed a slurry resembling cement.

Keri took out measuring tools and some white powder, using them to mark the area for the Chicken House on the ground.

The crops were growing, and there was almost no work to handle at the ranch, so D, with nothing else to do, brought a small stool to the riverside, fishing while watching the construction team work.

Seeing him fishing, Koraidon wanted to use Collision Course to help but was stopped.

Uncomprehendingly, Koraidon looked over.

D said leisurely, "No rush, I'm just here to pass the time."

Once the Chicken House is built, he would have new tasks to do.

---

---

---

---

# 22: Chapter 22: Cuisine Unfit for Human Consumption

While Keri was constructing the Chicken House, D wasn't idle either; he started to experiment with some special culinary techniques.

For example, the Green Grass Cake.

In the game, it's a special dish with the ability to change crop quality.

When players make this dish and feed it to the little people, the dishes cared for by them will undergo a magical mutation.

But… Can grass really be used to make a dish?

D stood in the kitchen area, rolled up his sleeves, got the utensils ready, and glanced at the grass brought by Koraidon and Cyclizar, some washed stones, and a piece of metal block shining with metallic luster.

It's well known that the types of Pokémon in this world are diverse, and their foods vary as well.

Some eat meat, eggs, and vegetables like humans, while others consume grass, stones, metal, emotions, or even souls.

So, he decided to try something bold.

Although limited by cookware, he couldn't make the Green Grass Cake, but different cooking methods can be applied to the same ingredient.

The early spring grass was exceptionally tender and lush.

After washing them clean with fresh water, removing the dirt and root stems, he put them in a pot and started steaming them with water.

"If I were a Grass Type Pokémon, I should prefer fresher grass." During the waiting period, D imagined himself as a Grass Type Pokémon.

So, the grass shouldn't be steamed too old or too dry; the moisture and natural juice must be retained as much as possible.

D calculated the time in his mind, and once five minutes had passed, he put on gloves and took out the porcelain plate containing the grass.

The steamed grass lay damply on the plate.

D looked down, and the information about the grass appeared in his mind.

[Steamed Green Grass (D-): Low-level dish, locking in the internal juice and moisture of the grass while enhancing its taste through steaming, it's more favored by some grass-eating Pokémon.

Cooking effect: After consumption, the chance for Grass Type Pokémon to comprehend Grass Type Moves is slightly increased.

Evaluation: Though inedible for humans, this dish is a rare delicacy for Pokémon. Your bold attempt will create an unprecedented path in the Pokémon world! Someday, your magical cuisine will spread to all Pokémon in this world, making them go crazy over it! Keep it up! Strive to create dishes that make Pokémon feel happy and blissful!]

D: "..."

Moving his gaze from that lengthy nonsense, D was shocked looking at the grass on the white porcelain plate before him.

Although he had anticipated it, seeing this scene now, he couldn't help but be astonished.

You can actually cook with grass! And it's not the lowest rank F- either!

So the question arises, can stones and metal blocks be used too?

D had a bold idea.

He looked at the metal block that gleamed with metallic luster and decided to try a different cooking technique.

He heated the oil in a pan, placed the metal block in when it was hot, then added various seasonings centered around chili peppers. Once the metal block was enveloped in red chili oil, he immediately plated it.

D looked down at it.

[Spicy Iron Block (D): Low-level dish, due to the hardness of the ingredient and its spicy taste, normal Pokémon can't eat it, but maybe a particular Pokémon might fancy it.

Cooking effect: After consumption, it slightly improves the control Pokémon have over Fire Type and Steel Type energy.

Additional effect: Feeding Heatran slightly increases its favorability.

Evaluation: @の#%&amp;amp;amp;amp;***!]

What the heck is this evaluation?

D was a bit confused, but seeing the effect above made him feel "as expected."

Metal can be cooked just like grass.

Then, perhaps those ordinary rocks by the roadside can be used too.

After all, Pokémon like Onix and Graveler eat the rocks found in mountains.

However, what surprised D the most was the additional effect of the "Spicy Iron Block."

Increase Heatran's favorability?

Speaking of Heatran, one has to mention the distant Sinnoh Region and the ancient myths circulating there.

According to legend, the Creator God Arceus created this world.

To provide this world with material and spiritual bases, he also created the Pokémon Dialga, who controls time, Palkia, who controls space, and the three lake spirits Azelf, Uxie, and Mesprit, who provide emotion, wisdom, and knowledge.

And during this process, Heatran was born.

In ancient times, during the battle between Dialga and Palkia, many fragments fell, and those fragments on the ground became Heatran.

"But Heatran should be in the Sinnoh Region now, right?" D pondered.

According to game settings, besides Sinnoh, there are Heatran in the Hoenn Region too.

But currently, he couldn't possibly go to either region, even if he did, he wouldn't know Heatran's exact location.

Thinking of this, D looked a bit regretfully at the Spicy Iron Block before him.

He turned and saw Koraidon and Cyclizar eagerly waiting beside him, he teased them: "Do you want to eat?"

Koraidon and Cyclizar looked at the piece covered in red chili oil and showed difficult expressions, shaking their heads in unison, rare as it was for them to agree on something.

D regretfully dumped the stones and grass into the trash can.

In the short term, these two special dishes couldn't be used.

However, when he gets the chance, he'll definitely visit the Sinnoh Region.

"Fire Steel" Heatran, that's a Legendary Beast!

Though considered a lower-tier Legendary Beast, it's still much rarer than Caterpie and other Pokémon.

As a former collector, D felt a collection urge stirring within him.

To gather all the cute, fluffy, rare Pokémon in the world and raise them in a ranch, watching them while farming; what a wonderful life it would be!

Hehehe!

Suddenly, D noticed there were two big fellows watching him.

He cleared his throat, reined in his smile, and turned to stroke the heads of Koraidon and Cyclizar, then walked out of the house to where Keri was building the Chicken House.

"Miss Keri, do you know where I can find Eevee, Vulpix, Pikachu, Skitty, Pachirisu, Espurr, those Pokémon?" D looked at her with hopeful eyes.

While the crops haven't grown yet, things are leisurely at the moment, he could take Koraidon and Cyclizar to bring one back.

Though Koraidon and Cyclizar have gentle and adorable personalities, silly as they may be, their size is too large.

Besides them, D wished to raise some small-sized, soft fluffy Pokémon that can fit in the bed and sleep with him.

Come the cold winter, with snow outside, while the fireplace is lit in the house, Koraidon and Cyclizar lie by the bedside, and little ones like Eevee, Vulpix, Skitty snuggle on his duvet, bed-ends, pillow edges, or in his arms.

This kind of life, even if offered an emperor's seat, wouldn't replace it!

---

---

---

---

# 23: Chapter 23: Chickens

"Eevee, Vulpix, Pikachu?"

Keri pondered for a moment and then shook her head, saying, "Sorry, I haven't seen them around here. The forest near the carpenter shop is inhabited by Pokemon like Tarountula, Nymble, and Smoliv."

"Alright."

Upon hearing this, D felt a bit disappointed, but he knew that some things can't be rushed. As long as he explores more in the future, he'll eventually encounter these Pokemon.

Time flew by, and before he knew it, three days had passed.

Today was the day the Chicken House was completed.

D stood in front of the small house built from wood and stone, gazing at the scene inside.

The house wasn't large, and the ground inside was covered with a layer of yellowing pasture grass. The grass mixed with soil had been pressed into a special surface using a professional machine.

On the left side of the house was a row of feed containers, next to which was a water trough for the hens to drink from when they're thirsty.

To the left of the containers was the place for grass and feed, connected to an outside silo.

That's right, Keri had generously gifted a silo during the Chicken House construction.

The silo was tall and large, built with red bricks and stones, making it quite eye-catching in the ranch.

In the corner of the house, there was a pile of thick hay and a pitchfork, alongside a special circular grass nest, surrounded by wooden boards.

"That is..."

With the construction work completed, Conkeldurr and Machamp began moving tools and a rock crusher into the truck.

Keri stayed behind and started introducing the Chicken House to D.

"That's the place for hatching eggs, also called an incubator. After placing the eggs in there, the hens will automatically go to hatch them, and after some time, chicks will be born.

If you don't want to buy every hen from the town, hatching them yourself is a good option."

Ah, understood! D nodded his head.

Keri walked into the house and continued explaining, "Every morning, you need to take feed from the silo and place it in these wooden troughs, then fill the water troughs. The chicks will eat on their own.

However, be cautious that while you can put more feed, never put less. If the chicks are not full, they'll be unhappy, affecting the quality of their eggs.

Additionally, on sunny days, you can open the Chicken House door and let the chicks out to graze, but make sure to fence the area around, otherwise, the chicks might wander off and get lost."

D nodded, remembering all this in his mind.

A wooden fence...

He could build it himself, but he didn't have much experience.

At that moment, Keri smiled and said, "I just made a new batch of wooden fences in the store. If you need, I can help fence the area around the Chicken House door."

D: "…How much?"

Keri: "Not much, just 1500 League coins."

Hearing this, D sighed with relief; the price of 1500 was indeed affordable. He hadn't done much fishing these past few days and had barely made any income, leaving him with around 16000 in savings after completing the Chicken House.

This money also needed to be used for buying chicks later in the afternoon, so if the price of the wooden fences was too high, he'd really have to think it over.

1500 was a price he could accept.

Decisively paying the money, Keri waved her hand, and two Machamps began using their muscular four arms to transport wooden fences from the truck.

Seeing this, D couldn't help but say, "You had this prepared all along."

Keri chuckled softly, "Because I knew you'd definitely buy it!"

D: "…"

Keri took a hammer from Conkeldurr's hand and cooperated with Machamp to fence the Chicken House with wooden fences.

When the last wooden fence was hammered into the ground, she pointed to the area opposite the Chicken House door and said:

"You can build a water mill there. With it, you can grind flour and make chicken feed on your own. Hmm, the price isn't expensive either, just 15,000 League coins, and we'll help prepare the required materials and facilities."

D: "…"

He suddenly realized that Keri had everything more thoroughly planned than he did, leaving D unsure of what to say for a moment.

Seeing his expression, Keri gave him a merchant's smile, saying, "If you're ready, come to the carpenter shop to find me! See you later!"

With that, she got into the truck, driven away by the Machamp who had learned driving skills, leaving the ranch with her.

Watching the truck disappear, D turned to look at the newly constructed Chicken House in the ranch.

Regardless, the Chicken House was built, and it was time to start his breeding endeavors!

That afternoon, D left Cyclizar at home and rode Koraidon to Porto Marinada Town—something he had promised Koraidon last time.

Koraidon was so happy it almost flew, sprinting joyfully along the narrow countryside paths.

Its speed far surpassed Cyclizar's, and it took just under ten minutes to bring D to Porto Marinada Town.

Koraidon transformed into a sprinting stance, following beside D.

On the streets, there were occasionally trainers passing by with their Pokemon, so people weren't too surprised by this situation, although they were curious, casting glances at Koraidon.

After all, it was a Pokemon they hadn't seen before.

D pretended not to notice, quickly heading to the ranch in town.

Although it was a ranch, it didn't rely on breeding for livelihood but mainly on selling ordinary animals and farm Pokemon.

Due to the nature of this kind of selling being different from the black market sale of Pokemon, the League permitted this type of transaction openly.

The ranch was called Happy Ranch, with a sign depicting a wide-grinning Miltank.

D entered the room with Koraidon, where the shopkeeper was a kindly old man with white hair, wearing reading glasses and looking down at a newspaper.

Upon hearing footsteps from the door, the old man looked up and was startled by Koraidon's massive figure.

However, upon seeing D next to Koraidon, he relaxed, touched his chest, and gasped, "Gave me a fright, thought a wild Pokemon had burst into the shop! Are you here to buy ranch animals?"

D was somewhat surprised, "How do you know?"

The old man wore a proud expression, "Well, Mayor Thomas told me recently that a new rancher had moved over, and that youngster should be you, right?"

D nodded slightly, "That's me."

"Haha!" The old man laughed, "I guessed it right, didn't I?"

He folded the newspaper, put it aside, and stood up, leading the way:

"Since that's the case, come with me to the back of the ranch and see which animals and Pokemon you need!"

---

---

# 24: Chapter 24: Delicious Alcremie (Double Chapter)

Passing through the back door, there appeared a section enclosed by a transparent glass dome ahead.

It somewhat resembled the backyard of Professor Oak's Research Institute from anime, complete with rivers, grasslands, trees, caves, and lakes.

Some Pokemon raised by humans frolicked here without a care, or sat in the shade of the trees enjoying the coolness.

D's gaze swept over them, silently recounting the names of these Pokemon: "Miltank, Wooloo, Smoliv, Nacli, Garganacl, Alcremie..."

Without a doubt, these were all Pokemon that could be raised on the ranch.

The kindly old man with graying temples, slightly stooped, clasped his hands behind his back, leading the way while introducing D to the Pokemon and ordinary animals at Happy Ranch.

"These kids were all raised by me personally. Each of them is very healthy and well-behaved, looking forward to being brought back to the ranch by a new owner."

Sure enough, the playful Pokemon cast curious glances at D, who was following behind the elderly man.

D was somewhat surprised to see the group of Nacli gathered around the Garganacl and couldn't help but ask, "Can Nacli be raised as well?"

"Of course!" the old man chuckled and said, "Don't underestimate them. In ancient times, they were incredibly important Pokemon. For the humans living on this land, these kids were indispensable."

D recalled the characteristics of Nacli and asked, "Because they can produce salt?"

"Exactly!" the old man nodded, "Nacli's body is composed of very precious rock salt. As long as they walk on the ground, their bodies get worn down, leaving a trail of salt."

"Collecting this salt yields a bottle of extremely precious rock salt. Of course, this doesn't harm their bodies at all, as they can always generate new salt within their bodies at any time if they wish to replenish it."

"When they evolve into Naclstack and Garganacl, the salt becomes even more nutritious and rich in minerals. Just sprinkle the salt produced by Garganacl on an injured Pokemon, and no matter how severe the wound, it will heal."

"Moreover, food prepared with this salt tastes even more delicious, and their selling price is much higher than the MooMoo Milk produced by Miltank!"

Upon hearing this, D understood in his heart and then inquired about the price of Nacli: "How much do they cost?"

Old man: "One Nacli costs 10,000 League Coins."

Ten thousand...

It sounded a bit expensive, but D calculated the value that Nacli could bring and couldn't help but feel that this price was quite reasonable.

With a Nacli, he wouldn't need to buy salt from outside anymore, and besides using it himself, he could also sell it outside, recouping his investment quickly.

However, even so, this price is still a bit steep for the currently financially straitened D.

He clicked his tongue slightly, fiddling with the bills in his pocket, and finally sighed.

Alas! I'll wait until this season's crops mature.

In the meantime, I can try my luck in the back hills, see if I can find any rare mountain goods to sell.

However, whether it's fishing or looking for mountain goods, it's not sustainable and relies on luck.

Reality isn't like a game; the number of fish in a body of water is limited, and after large-scale fishing, it becomes difficult to catch fish from there again.

Unless I switch to a different water body.

A map of the area near the ranch appeared in D's mind.

To the west of the ranch, crossing through Porto Marinada Town, lay the endless Paldea Sea, with even richer seafood resources.

To the north of the ranch, crossing the back hills and another forest, you could reach Casseroya Lake, where you can catch more freshwater fish.

"Luckily, there is still some time until the crops mature. I can have Koraidon take me to these two places for a look,"

D contemplated his next plans in his heart.

If he remembered correctly, Casseroya Lake was known for sightings of the very rare Dragonite lineage, Dratini.

Once Dratini evolves into Dragonite, it's not only intelligent but also powerful.

In the future, as the ranch prospers, it could also expand its offline business, with townspeople directly ordering fresh milk from the ranch.

By then, Dragonite, with its incredible speed, could help with deliveries.

Led by the old man, D strolled around the ranch and encountered some very special Pokemon.

For instance, Miltank, which produces milk; Wooloo, which has fluffy wool; Smoliv, capable of producing olive oil; Noibat, which helps pick berries; and Alcremie, which produces fresh cream.

Alcremie, made entirely of cream, emitted a fragrant, sweet smell of milk, which alone could evoke a strong sense of happiness in anyone who smelled it.

As he approached, D swallowed his saliva several times.

His actions were noticed by the Alcremie, which immediately shook in fear and hid behind a tree, worried it might get eaten if it didn't.

The old man chuckled softly upon seeing this and comforted the Alcremie: "Don't be afraid; this is the new rancher from town."

Pacified by the old man's reassurance, Alcremie's fear subsided significantly.

The old man laughed and told D: "It's a very special Pokemon indeed! So far, 63 forms of Alcremie have been confirmed, each producing cream of unique flavors.

---

"For example, the cream this little guy makes is vanilla-flavored, and over there is the Alcremie that can create milky caramel-flavored cream!"

Hearing this made D hungry.

After taking a stroll, the two of them returned to the front desk.

The elderly man took a price list out of the drawer and said, "See what kind of animals and Pokemon you want to buy?"

D looked down and saw that the price for each Pokemon was clearly marked.

Regular animals: Rooster 600, Hen 800, Duck 800

Pokemon: Combee 2500, Smoliv 2500, Alcremie 2600, Noibat 3000, Wooloo 10000, Miltank 20000, Nacli 10000

Feed: Chicken feed 50/portion, Cow and sheep feed 100/portion, Sweet feed 100/portion, Salty feed 100/portion

Looking at the prices, D asked, "About how much is in a portion of feed?"

"About two kilograms. This feed is made using berries, edible salt, flour, eggs, and other ingredients, with one portion enough to feed a Pokemon for half a month."

The elderly man looked down at the content and introduced each one:

"Chicken feed is made from cornmeal mixed with grains, and one portion lasts for a month, while cow and sheep feed is dry hay. The Miltank and Wooloo don't eat much, so one portion lasts about half a month."

"As for Combee and Noibat, they don't need you to provide food. As long as you give them a place to stay, they'll find food themselves. Noibat will bring back various fruits and berries for you."

"It's different for Combee. They live in beehives, and you need a flower field on the ranch to keep them."

"Once you have a flower field, they'll collect nectar daily and bring it back to the hive to make Honey."

D nodded, understanding in his heart.

"I currently have about fourteen thousand five hundred left in savings, which can buy ten hens and one rooster."

D originally planned like this, saving the rest for buying groceries.

But now, looking at those Pokemon, he decided to alter his plan.

Reducing the number of hens, he first purchased one Alcremie and one Smoliv.

Alcremie can produce cream, which helps him make Green Grass Cakes.

And Smoliv can produce olive oil, saving him money on buying oil.

This way, he's left with 9400.

"Hmm, then I'll buy three hens and one rooster, plus four portions of chicken feed and three portions of sweet feed should be enough."

The remaining money is for any unexpected needs.

Thinking of this, D made his decision: "I'll buy three hens and one rooster, one Alcremie and one Smoliv."

The elderly man nodded with a smile, "Alright, I'll have someone help deliver these to your ranch later."

D: "The location is..."

He was about to give the detailed location of the ranch, but the elderly man shook his head, "No need, I know the place."

D was a bit surprised, "Oh?"

A reminiscent smile appeared on the elderly man's face, "I was friends with your grandfather. When he was alive, I often visited him there. I even held you when you were little, though you probably don't remember now."

"I see!" D realized.

At this point, the elderly man squatted down, rummaging in the counter for a while before finally taking out a clear glass jar.

He handed over the glass jar, "This is Rock Salt produced by the Garganacl, take it back and give it a try!"

D looked at the glass jar.

Inside was a jar full of delicate, pink-colored salt grains.

[Purifying Salt: A top-quality seasoning produced by the Garganacl's fingertips, rich in minerals and trace elements, possessing magical wound-healing powers.

The Garganacl sprinkles it on injured Pokemon, and in an instant, the Pokemon's injuries heal.

As a result, many Pokemon gather around the Garganacl to lick its mineral-rich salt.

A very precious seasoning, it sells for a high price, adding special effects of "Stamina Recovery" to dishes.]

"This... is too valuable."

D instinctively shook his head.

But the elderly man said peacefully, "It's alright, consider it a gift to celebrate the reopening of the ranch. Your grandfather passed away early, and since I was good friends with him, taking care of you is the least I can do."

The elderly man's attitude was resolute.

At a time like this, refusing would be inappropriate.

D nodded and sincerely thanked him.

Only then did a smile appear on the elderly man's face, "D, right? Your grandfather would surely want to see the ranch return to its former glory, so keep it up! If you have any questions, feel free to come find me here."

D agreed, bid farewell to the elderly man, then turned and left the shop, riding Koraidon back to the ranch.

Soon after, a round-faced girl, about sixteen years old, with pigtails and a few freckles on her face, arrived at the ranch driving a cargo-laden tricycle.

Seeing D waiting there, she hesitantly asked, "Are you the new rancher my grandpa mentioned, Mr. D?"

D nodded slightly, "That's me."

The girl breathed a sigh of relief, parked the tricycle on the grass, then gently clapped her hands, revealing two small, cute Pokemon from the box.

On the left was a Pokemon with a body of pale pink, covered in sweet cream, with two items on its head resembling strawberries.

---

The one on the right was a Pokemon that resembled an olive.

Its body was a tender green, with a few green leaves growing on its head, and a petiole above its face. In the middle of the leaves, there was a yellow fruit.

They both showed only half their heads, looking somewhat frightened and worriedly at D not far away.

Seeing this, the girl said, "Don't worry, Mr. D is a kind person, he will definitely take good care of you!"

"Maa... Maa..."

"Fuu Fuu..."

The two little ones were still a bit scared.

But it seemed they weren't scared of D, but of the terrifyingly fierce (in the eyes of Alcremie and Smoliv) giant beast squatting beside him, with its entire body crimson red.

The girl was in a bit of a dilemma seeing this: "I'm really sorry, Mr. D."

D: "..."

He followed Alcremie and Smoliv's gaze and saw Koraidon beside him, immediately understanding what they were afraid of.

Clearing his throat lightly, D decided to send Koraidon off to do something else. He patted Koraidon's head and said to it, "Go see where Cyclizar has gone, and call it back home to welcome the new friends."

Cyclizar was happily chasing butterflies in the grass on the other side of the ranch. D knew this but didn't mind sending Koraidon to look for it.

"Graa!" Koraidon obediently turned around to find Cyclizar.

Seeing it leave, Alcremie and Smoliv visibly breathed a sigh of relief.

"Fuu..."

"Maa..."

The girl finally understood what Alcremie and Smoliv were afraid of.

Seeing the unfamiliar Pokemon leave, she encouraged, "Go ahead!"

Only then did Alcremie and Smoliv jump off the car, cautiously moving in front of D.

To avoid giving them a sense of pressure, D intentionally squatted down, reaching out to pat the two Pokemon's heads, saying, "Please take care of me, Smoliv, Alcremie!"

Pokemon are naturally pure and kind-hearted, and feeling the goodwill expressed by the human before them, happy smiles appeared on their faces.

The girl stepped forward and said, "I'll leave them to you, Mr. D, please take good care of them!"

D nodded, "I will."

The girl glanced at Smoliv and said, "By the way, Mr. D, my grandfather asked me to tell you something. The oil produced by Smoliv before it evolves into Dolliv is very bitter and not suitable for consumption. Only after it evolves into Dolliv does the oil become delicious."

"This Smoliv has been hatched for nearly a year, and it's almost time for it to evolve."

D was slightly taken aback and then nodded, "I see."

The girl quietly breathed a sigh of relief, then turned and carried down several large boxes of feed from the tricycle, finally placing a cage with three hens and a rooster on the ground.

"Excuse me, where is the Chicken House?"

D first placed Alcremie and Smoliv into the wooden house, letting them familiarize themselves with the environment, then stepped forward to lift the wooden boxes, "Allow me!"

"Ah? Thank you!" the girl quickly expressed her thanks and then followed with a box of feed.

With such a large cage, it was quite difficult for one girl to lift it, so D went ahead and carried the cage to the Chicken House.

As soon as the door opened, the hens foolishly ran out, wandering around inside the Chicken House, sniffing the grass, pecking at the ground, seemingly getting familiar with the environment.

The girl placed the feed box on the ground, and D opened the box, pouring the feed inside into the silo through the feeding trough, so that it could be easily accessed for feeding directly from inside.
